# AUDIT REPORT: Grade 6 HTML practice studios against the canonical lessons

Generated from `data/audit/studio-audit/<lesson>.json` by `scripts/audit/build-audit-report.mjs`. Method and class definitions: `docs/specs/studio-audit.md`.

## Fixed since this audit

Findings from the audit that have been fixed in code, with the proof. Everything else in the lesson-by-lesson table is still open.

- **Mastery questions lost their scenario and picture when generated from a lesson's Connect section (2-2 to 2-5, 2-9 to 2-12, 3-2, 3-4, 3-5, 3-8, 3-9, 5-x, 6-1 to 6-6, 6-9 to 6-15, 7-2 to 7-8, 8-x, 9-x)**  
  Fix: The converter now carries the Connect scenario onto each Connect question, and attaches the Connect figure when it shows what the problem GIVES (histogram, dot plot, box plot, coordinate points). Bar charts, tapes and number lines are not attached because they show the worked result.  
  Proof: scripts/lib/practice-studio-config.mjs (diagramToViz, GIVEN); tools/studio-viz.test.mjs now renders every figure on every generated page (2,026).
- **7-1 integerOpposites: the stem printed the tick count while the figure plotted tick x unit (17% of level-3 draws)**  
  Fix: oppLetters prints the plotted number for integer lines.  
  Proof: tools/studio-gen-figures.test.mjs checks 82 stems against their figures; it fails on the old code.
- **7-7 polygonCoordinates: the missing-vertex figure drew a diagonal and left out a side (135 of 291 draws); the opposite-corner variant labelled all four corners**  
  Fix: Segments join only vertices that are neighbours around the rectangle; the opposite-corner figure labels only P and Q.  
  Proof: tools/studio-gen-figures.test.mjs checks 582 drawn segments are sides.
- **2-2 histograms drew gapped bars for bins written as inclusive pairs (20-24, 25-29)**  
  Fix: The hist figure draws such bins touching; the printed ranges and the text alternative keep the authored pairs.  
  Proof: tools/studio-viz.test.mjs asserts adjacent bars touch.
- **Typed table cells were graded by an exact string or only the leading number: 'Median' failed against a sentence key, '9' passed for '9 to 15', '5n' failed**  
  Fix: RT.util.cellOk: normalizes spacing, case, minus and multiplication signs and 'to' vs ':'; accepts 'a or b' alternatives; a key with a second number must match whole; the converter adds the short answer to long sentence keys.  
  Proof: tools/studio-table-cells.test.mjs (37 cases).
- **ASCII hyphen printed as the minus sign and 'x' as multiplication in authored and converted text (Units 6-8)**  
  Fix: Display text is typographized at build time (U+2212, ×); answers, ids and figure specs are untouched.  
  Proof: scripts/lib/math-typography.mjs; 7-1 now prints 95 true minus signs and no hyphen negatives.

## Summary

72 lessons (Units 2-9), 925 widgets classified.

| Class | Widgets | Share | Lessons whose worst widget is this class |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| ALIGNED | 713 | 77% | 5 |
| QUESTIONABLE | 49 | 5% | 5 |
| MISALIGNED | 74 | 8% | 7 |
| REDUNDANT | 14 | 2% | 3 |
| BROKEN-MODEL | 75 | 8% | 52 |

Aligned lessons: 5 of 72. Lessons with at least one MISALIGNED or BROKEN-MODEL widget: 59.

## Formulas used before they are taught

- 2-2: median of the data (interval containing the median) used in 2-2, taught in 2-3
- 2-3: mean = sum ÷ count used in 2-3, taught in 2-8
- 2-3: range = maximum − minimum used in 2-3, taught in 2-5
- 2-4: range = maximum − minimum used in 2-4, taught in 2-5
- 3-4: y = kx (as 'rule: y = 4x' and 'y = 2.5 × x') used in 3-4, taught in 9-3
- 4-1: Part = Percent × Whole (percent of a number, benchmarks, discount) used in 4-1, taught in 4-4
- 4-1: Whole = Part ÷ Percent (whole from part and percent) used in 4-1, taught in 4-5
- 4-1: fraction / decimal / percent conversion rules (×100, ÷100, divide numerator by denominator) used in 4-1, taught in 4-2
- 4-2: Part = Percent × Whole (percent of a quantity) used in 4-2, taught in 4-4
- 5-1: A = ½ × b × h (triangle), as a 'triangle rule' in Practice §3.2 and in choice feedback used in 5-1, taught in 5-2
- 5-5: volume with fractional edge lengths (V = B × h with fractions) used in 5-5, taught in 5-10
- 6-1: dividing by a fraction = multiplying by its reciprocal (keep-change-flip) used in 6-1, taught in 6-2
- 6-3: order of operations with grouping symbols and powers (PEMDAS) used in 6-3, taught in 6-4
- 6-4: variable and coefficient (2.50 + 1.75m) used in 6-4, taught in 6-5
- 6-4: solving for an unknown in an equation (6 × □² − 4 = 92) used in 6-4, taught in 8-1
- 6-5: solving an equation for the variable (5t + 8 = 58, w² + 12 = 48) used in 6-5, taught in 8-3
- 6-6: a(b + c) = ab + ac (distributive property: expand and factor) used in 6-6, taught in 6-14
- 6-7: GCF from the shared primes of a prime factorization used in 6-7, taught in 6-13
- 6-7: factoring out the GCF, ab + ac = a(b + c) used in 6-7, taught in 6-14
- 6-8: a(b + c) = ab + ac (expanding with algebra tiles and L4 combine draws) used in 6-8, taught in 6-14
- 7-1: adding signed integers (net result of −10, 4, −3, 21) used in 7-1, taught in n/a (not taught in Grade 6 units 2-9)
- 7-1: ordering integers least to greatest used in 7-1, taught in 7-4
- 7-2: |x| absolute value ('|−1/4| = 0.25') and distance from 0 used in 7-2, taught in 7-3
- 7-2: inequality chain (−4 < −1 < 0 < 2) used in 7-2, taught in 7-4
- 7-3: |x − a| = d (numbers d units from a number other than 0, i.e. distance between two points) used in 7-3, taught in 7-6
- 7-5: quadrant sign patterns I (+, +), II (−, +), III (−, −), IV (+, −) used in 7-5, taught in 7-8
- 7-6: rectangle perimeter 2(l + w) and area l × w read from vertex coordinates used in 7-6, taught in 7-7
- 7-8: quadrant sign patterns I (+, +), II (−, +), III (−, −), IV (+, −) used in 7-5, taught in 7-8
- 8-1: x + a = b → x = b − a (subtract the same number from both sides) used in 8-1, taught in 8-2
- 8-1: x ÷ a = b → x = a × b (multiplication undoes division) used in 8-1, taught in 8-3
- 8-2: x + a = b → x = b − a; x − a = b → x = b + a used in 8-1, taught in 8-2
- 9-1: two-variable equation (y = kx, y = x + c; e.g. c = 3t, d = m + 4, y = 15x as a table rule) used in 9-1, taught in 9-3
- 9-1: plotting ordered pairs (x, y) from a table on a coordinate plane used in 9-1, taught in 9-2
- 9-2: letter equation as the rule of a relationship (c = 45t, c = 3m, a = b + 4) used in 9-2, taught in 9-3
- 9-2: choosing an equation that matches a graph (y = kx, y = x + c) used in 9-2, taught in 9-3

## Lessons whose canonical model students cannot manipulate in the studio

- 2-3 Describe the Data Using the Median: ordered list / dot plot where the median is the middle value found by counting in from both ends (mean of the two middle values for an even count)
- 2-11 Add and Subtract Decimals: vertical addition and subtraction with the decimal points lined up in place-value columns and zeros annexed
- 3-2 Understand Rates and Unit Rates: unit rate as equal groups (one per item) and a double number line for a constant rate such as travel time
- 4-3 Estimate the Percent of a Number: double number line (amount above, percent below) marked in benchmark jumps of 10%, 25%, 50%, with compatible numbers chosen first
- 4-4 Find and Compare with Percentages: tape diagram / double number line for the percent of a whole, and the equation Part = Percent × Whole with the percent written as a decimal
- 4-5 Determine the Whole Given the Part and Percent: double number line (known part under its percent, step to 10% or 1%, count up to 100%) and the equation Whole = Part ÷ Percent
- 5-6 Represent Three-Dimensional Figures in Two Dimensions: net of a prism or pyramid that folds without overlap, faces read off the net and added
- 5-7 Determine Surface Area of Prisms: net or oblique drawing of a rectangular or triangular prism, faces added (two triangles plus three rectangles)
- 5-8 Determine Surface Area of Pyramids: net of a pyramid (base polygon plus triangular faces whose height is the slant height)
- 5-9 Area of Regular Polygons: regular polygon cut from its center into n congruent triangles whose height is the apothem
- 6-8 Generate Equivalent Expressions: rearranging and regrouping terms or factors without changing the value (4 × 17 × 25 = 4 × 25 × 17 = 100 × 17), and writing the identity element (a + 0, a × 1)
- 8-5 Understand Inequalities and Their Solutions: number-line graph: a circle on the boundary (open or closed) and a shaded ray toward the solutions
- 9-1 Explore Relationships Between Two Variables: input/output table and the sentence '(dependent) depends on (independent)': the quantity you choose versus the quantity that responds

|  |  |  | Challenge §3 items 3.1-3.2 (act:nline mode 'place' L3, L4): place 2 3/4 and −0.2 on a number line | MISALIGNED | Same mismatch as Practice 4.7 (no alignment or regrouping is practised), and L4 asks for a negative decimal (for example 'Place a point at −0.2') although negative numbers are not taught until Unit 7. | Add a new act (assets/studio-act/decimalcols.js, kind 'decimalcols', mode 'addsub'): a vertical-arithmetic board with fixed columns tens \| ones \| decimal point \| tenths \| hundredths \| thousandths. The student drags each addend's digits into the grid (the point column is fixed, so the point lands in place), taps an empty cell to annex a grey 0, then fills the answer column by column from the right with a carry/regroup box above each column. L1 same number of places; L2 different places (annex a zero); L3 subtraction with regrouping across a zero (12.5 − 3.45, 30 − 12.75); L4 error trap: a pre-set board with the addends right-aligned (6.4 over 2.35, the 4 under the 5) that the student must repair by sliding one addend. Grade the alignment (every point in the point column) and then the sum; name the right-edge-alignment misconception ('you lined up the last digits, not the points') and the missing-annexed-zero misconception ('a gap is not a 0'). Text bilingual like the other acts. |

|  |  |  | Practice §3 item 3.2, §5 item 5.3; Challenge §5 item 5.2 (tbl, mc, num): Friendly-number rearrangements: 25 × 7 × 4 → 25 × 4 × 7 = 100 × 7, 19 + 36 + 1, 50 × 9 × 2, 4 × 17 × 25, 5 × 37 × 20 | REDUNDANT | The lesson's idea is to reorder and regroup to make friendly numbers, which is a drag-and-regroup move, but in the studio the rearrangement is already printed (3.2 shows '25 × 4 × 7 = 100 × 7' and asks only for 700) or is one multiple-choice stem (5.3 4 × 17 × 25 = 1,700; C 5.2 asks for the product). Nothing lets the student perform or justify the rearrangement. | New act:regroup (assets/studio-act/regroup.js): a row of movable term/factor chips for an expression such as 4 × 17 × 25; dragging chips swaps order (log 'commutative') and tapping two adjacent chips draws a bracket (log 'associative'); a running line shows the current form and a bracket total (4 × 25 = 100); the task ends when the student has produced a form containing a power of ten product and typed the value (1,700). Graded by (a) every form equals the original value and (b) a friendly product was formed; wrong moves trapped: swapping across subtraction/division ('15 − 8' chips refuse to swap and say why) and changing numbers. Use it at L1 (+ chips), L2 (× chips), L3 (mixed with a variable). Replace P 3.2 and 5.3 and C 5.2 with it. |

Directly targets reading an equation, the first half of the objective. |  |

Caveat: the same generator and level settings also serve 9-1, so the studio does not distinguish the lessons. |  |
|  |  |  | Challenge 3.3 (match), 4.1, 4.2, 4.3 (error analysis), 2.6 (tbl), 5.6 (written): situation to equation; f = m + 6 for 6 liters every minute, d = 3c with the variables reversed, rate found by subtracting | ALIGNED | Pairs verified (9m, c + 4, 2.5d, 18h). Error step indices right; each fault is a lesson misconception (add instead of multiply, dependent not alone, rate from the difference). |  |
|  |  |  | Practice 4.7 (act:plot table L2); Challenge 3.2 (L4 table): given a rule such as d = 3m or a = b + 4, plot the pairs on a coordinate plane | ALIGNED | Equation-to-points direction: the student substitutes into the equation and plots, which reinforces 'every point on the graph satisfies the equation'. At L4 the table gives y and the student works back to x. It never asks the student to write the equation, so it supports rather than carries the objective. |  |
|  |  |  | Challenge 3.1 (act:plot ratio L3): 'The ratio of bracelets to beads is 2 : 3. Plot each pair from the table' | MISALIGNED | Ratio-table plotting with ratio wording and no equation. 9-3's objective is writing the equation; the activity never mentions variables, a rate or an equation. | Use act:plot table mode with the equation hidden: show the table and the scenario ('Each bracelet uses K beads'), ask the student to plot AND choose the equation (four options with the variables reversed or the rate added) before the Check button enables. |
|  |  |  | Practice 5.1, 5.2, 5.3; Challenge 2.2, 2.3 (mc): Diamond's two options: 'In Diamond's situation, what should be the independent variable in both equations?', 'Why does Diamond need TWO separate equations?', 'How does she use them to decide?'; Miguel's equation for a different gym; 'the benefit of an equation over a table for the membership costs' | BROKEN-MODEL | Diamond, her two options (buying versus streaming songs), Miguel's equation c = 24.95m and 'the membership costs' table are never shown. 5.2 and 5.3 cannot be answered without the unseen prices, and Challenge 2.3 refers to a table that is not on screen. Only the explanations mention them. | Put the scenario in the item: 'Diamond can buy songs at $1.29 each (c = 1.29n) or stream at $9 a month for any number of songs (c = 9)' with a two-column table viz for each option, then ask 5.1-5.3 over it. In Challenge 2.2 restate 'Miguel wrote c = 24.95m for Recreation World' and in 2.3 attach the membership table viz. |

|  |  |  | Challenge 2.3 (mc): 'The solution to one problem was 41.7 cars. Would producing or washing a PARTIAL car make sense?' | BROKEN-MODEL | The unseen problem decides the answer: if the council NEEDS at least 41.7 cars' worth, round up (the keyed answer), but if it can only AFFORD 41.7 cars, round down, which is distractor 3. With no problem on screen, two choices are defensible. | Give the problem: 'The council needs at least $500 at $12 a car, so 12n ≥ 500 and n ≈ 41.7 cars. What should it do about the 0.7 of a car?' with choices 'Round up to 42, because 41 cars raise only $492', 'Round down to 41, because 0.7 of a car cannot be washed', 'Wash 41.7 cars', 'The equation is wrong'. |
